- 2026-11-16 20:15 PST LOAD SPREADING finding before I write code: ot-run placement is ALREADY load-aware. It orders every host that can admit the job by projected load ((load1 + fresh threads + job threads) / cores, ot-run lines 751-763, v4/v6 least loaded first, never hold) and never refuses on load. Live proof just now: ot-run --pick --mem 20 --threads 8 with epyc10 at load 174/96 and intel1 at 21/112 chose intel1; epyc10 shows only 17 GB admit (45 running, 1 waiter). So an UNPINNED job does not land on epyc10. What keeps epyc10 at 190: work that can only run there because its source is only there: ot-run --dir locality (a job whose --dir exists only on epyc10 qualifies only epyc10) and kit_wave gen_host picked from die.gen_hosts (qwen_kvsys gen_hosts = [epyc10] only, HEAD/S81 now epyc10+epyc8). KV HG2c G0 crawling is that case. The structural fix is therefore tree replication, not a placement change: KV D10 -> epyc8 (waiting on KV-R) and the current S81/HEAD/KV trees to intel1 (17/112 load, 346 GB free) and epyc2/epyc7/epyc9 as disk allows. Proposal: I copy S81 f2s4 d3, HEAD NF15c and (when posted) KV D10 to intel1 with full verify and add intel1 to those gen_hosts, same pattern as epyc8. Also a small code change I can make if you still want it: kit_wave pick_gen_host chooses the least-loaded host of die.gen_hosts when several qualify (today first/lowest by its own rule); say yes and I branch+gate it. Needs your ok on stage A hardlinks vs the 11-16 rule (chmod/write through a shared inode would hit the base: trees are a-w, retire3 uses chmod -R u+w before rmtree, which is safe for unlinking). - 2026-11-17 15:27 PST ANSWER ot_wait.py is not mine: it is WAVE-FLOW-R2's (r591, branch claude/wave-flow-r2-otwait-1117 @014f1f8, merged as e47c3895cc5). Your diagnosis is right in effect, wrong in detail: it does read systemctl show, and classify_unit() ends in `if act == "failed" or res not in ("", "success"): FAILED` BEFORE it looks at the ot-run client log. A .scope has no ExecMainStatus (systemd never execs the job), and when its cgroup is torn down after the job exited it shows ActiveState=failed Result=success: checked live on epyc8 (oth-ubuntu-1035341-1794691162.scope: ActiveState=failed, Result=success). So a job that logged `ot-run: RAN on ... exit 0` was reported FAILED. I did the fix anyway because it is small and has a test: claude/fleet-ops-r-otwait-scope-1117 @48c7dbc1036903d5c4e3f4d19b4ad5d85e82b1e8 GATE main=8569788b032 pass=1/1 GREEN (record ~/merge-queue/gates/48c7dbc1036903d5c4e3f4d19b4ad5d85e82b1e8.json): for a scope (Type=scope or Id ending .scope) that is `failed` with Result success, DONE/FAILED follows the exit status the client log recorded (exit 0 -> DONE, exit 3 -> FAILED with rc 3), no logged exit -> FAILED with 'no exit status in the client log' (never DONE by guess); a service failure and a scope with a real non-success Result (oom-kill) stay FAILED even when the log says exit 0. Tests with a fake scope: the three cases plus the service/oom cases. WAVE-FLOW-R2 owns the file: please have them review/approve the branch (PROPS now also asks for Id).

(2) ot-heavy stale admission lock: on main @7c54d9c198c. Cause: flock lives while ANY process holds the inherited fd 8, so a child that outlived the holder kept admit.lock for 70 min. Now the holder writes its pid to admit.lock.pid; a waiter that has waited OT_HEAVY_LOCK_WAIT_S (30) and finds that pid gone replaces the lock FILE (new inode; the leaked fd keeps the dead one) under a break guard, and logs 'STALE-LOCK' to admit.log; a live holder is still waited for. Tests: free lock, leaked-fd dead holder broken in < 15 s, live holder not broken (+ the 34 existing ot-heavy tests pass). /usr/local/bin/ot-heavy points at rt/current on all hosts, so it reaches them with the rt sync (intel1 already has it; others on 83f293d3b85f, 7c54d9c is the next sync).
